             Additional information Communicated by S. Chatterjee. Publisher's Note Springer Nature remains neutral with regard to jurisdictional claims in published maps and institutional affiliations. Appendices Appendix A. Being in  \(\Omega _k\)  at Each Positive Time In this appendix, we prove a technical result on the probability that the  \(\varepsilon ^{-1}\) -accelerated  \(\mathrm SIP\)  sits in the absorbing set  \(\Omega _k\)  (see ( 5.4 )) at a given positive time. Since  \(\Omega _k = \Xi _k\)  when  \(k=1\) , we fix  \(k\ge 2\)  all throughout this appendix. We use the notation employed in Sect.  5 . Recall from the proof of Proposition  5.1  that  \(p_t^\varepsilon (\cdot ,\cdot )\)  denotes the transition kernel of the  \(\varepsilon ^{-1}\) -sped up  \(\textrm{SIP}(G,\varepsilon \hat{\alpha })\)  with  k  particles, i.e., $$\begin{aligned} p_t^\varepsilon (\eta ,\xi )= e^{t\varepsilon ^{-1}L_{\varepsilon \hat{\alpha },k}}\mathbb {1}_\xi (\eta )\; \text {,}\;\;\quad \eta ,\xi \in \Xi _k\; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ The following proposition is the main result of this appendix. 
                   Proposition A.1 
                   For any configuration  \(\eta \in \Xi _k\)  and time  \(t>0\) , we have $$\begin{aligned} \lim _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} p_t^\varepsilon (\eta ,\Omega _k) = 1 \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ 
                 To prove Proposition  A.1 , we need a series of technical lemmas. Let  \((\eta ^\varepsilon (t))_{t\ge 0}\)  be the  \(\varepsilon ^{-1}\) -sped up  \(\mathrm SIP\)  with generator  \(\varepsilon ^{-1}L_{\varepsilon \hat{\alpha },k}\) . Let  \(\mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \)  and  \(\mathbb {E}_\eta ^\varepsilon \)  denote the law and corresponding expectation of  \((\eta ^\varepsilon (t))_{t\ge 0}\)  starting from  \(\eta \in \Xi _k\) . The first lemma states that the time to reach the absorbing set  \(\Omega _k\)  from the transient collection  \(\Delta _k\)  (defined in ( 5.5 )) is uniformly negligible. Given a subset  A  of  \(\Xi _k\) , let  \(\tau _A\)  be its (random) first hitting time. Moreover, define $$\begin{aligned} c_\textrm{min} = \min _{\begin{array}{c} x,y\in V\\ c_{xy}>0 \end{array}} c_{xy}> 0 \; \text {,}\;\;\quad c_\textrm{max} = \max _{x,y\in V}c_{xy} \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ 
                    (A.1)
                 
                   Lemma A.2 
                   Suppose that  \(\varepsilon |\hat{\alpha }| \le \frac{c_\textrm{min}}{4\,c_\textrm{max}\,k}\) . Then, for some  \(C_1=C_1(G)>0\)  and for all  \(\varepsilon >0\) ,  \(k\ge 2\) , $$\begin{aligned} \max _{\zeta \in \Delta _k} \mathbb {E}_\zeta ^\varepsilon \,[ \tau _{\Omega _k}] \le \frac{{C_1}\varepsilon }{c_\textrm{min}} \, \log k \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ 
                
                   Proof 
                   Define a test function  \(\phi : \Xi _k \rightarrow \mathbb {R}\)  as $$\begin{aligned} \phi (\eta ) :=- \sum _{x\in V } \left( 1+\frac{1}{2} + \cdots + \frac{1}{\eta _x} \right) \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ Then, there exists  \(C_1=C_1(G)>0\)  such that $$\begin{aligned} \max _{\eta \in \Xi _k} \phi (\eta ) - \min _{\eta \in \Xi _k} \phi (\eta ) \le \frac{{C_1}}{4} \log k \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ 
                    (A.2)
                 For each  \(\zeta \in \Delta _k\) , we may express  \(L_{\varepsilon \hat{\alpha },k} \phi (\zeta )\)  as $$\begin{aligned} &  \sum _{ \{x,y \} \subseteq V} c_{xy} \big [ \zeta _x \,(\varepsilon \hat{\alpha }_y +\zeta _y ) \, ( \phi (\zeta -\delta _x +\delta _y ) - \phi (\zeta )) + \zeta _y \, (\varepsilon \hat{\alpha }_x + \eta _x ) \,( \phi (\zeta -\delta _y + \delta _x ) \nonumber \\ &  \quad - \phi (\zeta )) \big ] \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ 
                    (A.3)
                 If  \(\zeta _x, \zeta _y \ge 1\) , then the term inside the bracket in ( A.3 ) becomes $$\begin{aligned} \frac{\zeta _x}{\zeta _y+1} + \frac{\zeta _y}{\zeta _x+1} + \varepsilon \hat{\alpha }_y \left( 1 - \frac{\zeta _x}{\zeta _y+1} \right) + \varepsilon \hat{\alpha }_x \left( 1 - \frac{\zeta _y}{\zeta _x+1} \right) \ge \frac{ (1-\varepsilon \hat{\alpha }_y) \,\zeta _x}{\zeta _y+1} + \frac{ (1-\varepsilon \hat{\alpha }_x) \, \zeta _y}{\zeta _x+1} \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ By the assumption, we have  \(\varepsilon \hat{\alpha }_\textrm{max} \le \varepsilon |\hat{\alpha }| \le \frac{c_\textrm{min}}{4 \, c_\textrm{max} \, k} < \frac{1}{2}\) . Thus, the right-hand side is bounded from below by $$\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{2} \left( \frac{ \zeta _x}{\zeta _y+1} + \frac{ \zeta _y}{\zeta _x+1} \right) \ge \frac{1}{2} \; \text {,}\;\;\end{aligned}$$ 
                    (A.4)
                 where the inequality holds because  \(\zeta _x,\zeta _y \ge 1\) . Next, if  \(\zeta _x\ge 1\)  and  \(\zeta _y=0\) , then the term inside the bracket in ( A.3 ) equals $$\begin{aligned} \zeta _x \,\varepsilon \hat{\alpha }_y \left( \frac{1}{\zeta _x} - 1 \right) = - \varepsilon \hat{\alpha }_y \, (\zeta _x -1) \ge -\varepsilon \hat{\alpha }_y \, \zeta _x \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ 
                    (A.5)
                 Similarly, if  \(\zeta _x=0\)  and  \(\zeta _y\ge 1\) , then the term is at least  \(-\varepsilon \hat{\alpha }_x \, \zeta _y\) . As  \(\zeta \in \Delta _k\) , there exists at least one  \(\{x^*,y^*\}\)  such that  \(c_{x^*y^*}>0\)  and  \(\zeta _{x^*},\zeta _{y^*} \ge 1\) . Thus, by ( A.1 ), ( A.3 ), ( A.4 ), and ( A.5 ), $$\begin{aligned} L_{\varepsilon \hat{\alpha },k}\phi (\zeta ) \ge \frac{c_{x^*y^*}}{2} - \sum _{\begin{array}{c} x\in V \\ \zeta _x \ge 1 \end{array}} \sum _{\begin{array}{c} y\in V \\ \zeta _y = 0 \end{array}} c_{xy} \,\varepsilon \hat{\alpha }_y \,\zeta _x \ge \frac{c_\textrm{min}}{2} - c_\textrm{max} \,k\, \varepsilon |\hat{\alpha }| \ge \frac{c_\textrm{min}}{4} \; \text {,}\;\;\end{aligned}$$ 
                    (A.6)
                 where the last inequality holds since  \(\varepsilon |\hat{\alpha }| \le \frac{c_\textrm{min}}{4 \, c_\textrm{max} \, k}\) . 
                   Now, it is well known that $$\begin{aligned} \phi (\eta ^\varepsilon (t)) - \phi (\eta ^\varepsilon (0)) - \int _0^t ( \varepsilon ^{-1} L_{\varepsilon \hat{\alpha },k} \phi ) (\eta ^\varepsilon (s)) \, \textrm{d}s \; \text {,}\;\;\quad t\ge 0 \; \text {,}\;\;\end{aligned}$$ is a  \(\mathbb {P}_\zeta ^\varepsilon \) -martingale. Setting  \(t=\tau _{\Omega _k} \wedge r \) , applying ( A.2 ) and ( A.6 ), taking expectations with respect to  \(\mathbb {E}_\zeta ^\varepsilon \) , and then sending  \(r \rightarrow \infty \) , we obtain $$\begin{aligned} \frac{c_\textrm{min} \, \varepsilon ^{-1}}{4} \,\mathbb {E}_\zeta ^\varepsilon \, [\tau _{\Omega _k}] \le \mathbb {E}_\zeta ^\varepsilon \left[ \int _0^{\tau _{\Omega _k}} (\varepsilon ^{-1} L_{\varepsilon \hat{\alpha },k} \phi )(\eta ^\varepsilon (s)) \, \textrm{d}s \right] \le \frac{{C_1}}{4} \log k \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ This concludes the proof of the lemma.  \(\square \) 
                 For each  \(t\ge 0\) , let  \(T_\varepsilon (t)\)  be the local time spent by  \((\eta ^\varepsilon (s))_{s\ge 0}\)  in  \(\Omega _k\)  up to time  t : $$\begin{aligned} T_\varepsilon (t) :=\int _0^t \textbf{1}\{ \eta ^\varepsilon (s) \in \Omega _k \} \, \textrm{d}s \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ 
                    (A.7)
                 Then, let  \(S_\varepsilon (s)\) ,  \(s\ge 0\) , be its generalized inverse: $$\begin{aligned} S_\varepsilon (s) :=\sup \, \{ t \ge 0: T_\varepsilon (t) \le s \} \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ By definition,  \(T_\varepsilon (S_\varepsilon (s)) = s\)  and  \(T_\varepsilon (S_\varepsilon (s)+u)>s\)  for any  \(s\ge 0\)  and  \(u>0\) . In turn, we have $$\begin{aligned} \eta ^\varepsilon (S_\varepsilon (s)) \in \Omega _k\; \text {,}\;\;\quad s \ge 0 \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ 
                    (A.8)
                 Note also that  \(\{ S_\varepsilon (s) \ge s+u \} = \{ T_\varepsilon (s+u) \le s \}\)  for  \(s\ge 0\)  and  \(u>0\) . 
                   Lemma A.3 
                   For all  \(\eta \in \Xi _k\) ,  \(t\ge 0\)  and  \(\delta >0\) , we have $$\begin{aligned} \lim _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [ S_\varepsilon (t) \ge t+ \delta ] = 0 \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ 
                
                   Proof 
                   First, suppose that  \(\eta \in \Delta _k\) . We may bound $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [ S_\varepsilon (t) \ge t+\delta ]&= \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [ T_\varepsilon (t+\delta ) \le t ] \\&\le \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [ T_\varepsilon (t+\delta ) \le t , \ \tau _{\Omega _k} < \delta /2 ] + \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [ \tau _{\Omega _k} \ge \delta /2 ] \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ By Lemma  A.2  and the Markov inequality, we obtain $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [ \tau _{\Omega _k} \ge \delta /2 ] \le \frac{2}{\delta }\, \mathbb {E}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [ \tau _{\Omega _k}] \le \frac{2}{\delta }\, \frac{{C_1} \varepsilon }{c_\textrm{min}} \, \log k \xrightarrow {\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} 0 \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ Thus, by the strong Markov property of  \((\eta ^\varepsilon (s))_{s\ge 0}\)  and the fact that  \(t \mapsto T_\varepsilon (t)\)  is non-decreasing, we deduce $$\begin{aligned} \limsup _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [ S_\varepsilon (t) \ge t+\delta ] \le \limsup _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \sup _{\xi \in \Omega _k} \mathbb {P}_\xi ^\varepsilon \, [ T_\varepsilon ( t+ \delta /2 ) \le t] \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ Thus, it suffices to prove Lemma  A.3  for  \(\eta \in \Omega _k\) . 
                   It remains to prove that, for  \(\eta \in \Omega _k\) ,  \(t\ge 0\)  and  \(\delta >0\) , $$\begin{aligned} \lim _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [ T_\varepsilon (t+\delta ) \le t ] = 0 \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ Let  \(0<\tau _1< \tau _2 < \cdots \)  be the consecutive jump times from  \(\Omega _k\)  to  \(\Delta _k\) . Then, for any integer  \(m \ge 1\) , we get $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \,[ T_\varepsilon (t+\delta ) \le t ] \le \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \,[ T_\varepsilon (t+\delta ) \le t, \ \tau _{m+1} > t+\delta ] + \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \,[ \tau _{m+1} \le t + \delta ] \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ 
                    (A.9)
                 Conditioned on the first event in the right-hand side of ( A.9 ), there occurs at most  m  jumps from  \(\Omega _k\)  to  \(\Delta _k\)  up to time  \(t+\delta \) . Let  \(\tau _1'< \tau _2' < \cdots \)  denote the consecutive return times to  \(\Omega _k\) , such that we have $$\begin{aligned} 0< \tau _1< \tau _1'< \tau _2< \tau _2' < \cdots \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ Then, by ( A.7 ), we may bound the first probability in the right-hand side of ( A.9 ) by $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [ (\tau _1'-\tau _1) + (\tau _2' - \tau _2) + \cdots + (\tau _m' - \tau _m ) \ge \delta ] \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ We may further bound this by $$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \sum _{i=1}^m \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon [ \tau _i'-\tau _i \ge \delta /m ]&\le m \sup _{\zeta \in \Delta _k} \mathbb {P}_\zeta ^\varepsilon [ \tau _{\Omega _k} \ge \delta / m ] \\&\le \frac{m^2}{\delta } \sup _{\zeta \in \Delta _k} \mathbb {E}_\zeta ^\varepsilon [ \tau _{\Omega _k}] \le \frac{m^2 {C_1} \varepsilon \log k}{\delta c_\textrm{min}} \xrightarrow {\varepsilon \rightarrow 0 } 0 \; \text {.}\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$ 
                    (A.10)
                 Here, the first inequality is due to the strong Markov property at  \(\tau _i\)  for each  \(i \in \llbracket 1,m \rrbracket \) , the second inequality is due to the Markov inequality, and the third inequality is due to Lemma  A.2 . Further, for  \((\eta ^\varepsilon (s))_{s\ge 0}\) , any direct jump from  \( \Omega _k\)  to  \(\Delta _k\)  has rate bounded above by $$\begin{aligned} \sup _{\eta \in \Omega _k}\sup _{x,y \in V} \varepsilon ^{-1} \, c_{xy} \, \eta _x\varepsilon \hat{\alpha }_y \le c_\textrm{max} \, k \, \hat{\alpha }_\textrm{max} \; \text {,}\;\;\end{aligned}$$ where  \(\hat{\alpha }_\textrm{max} :=\max _{x\in V} \hat{\alpha }_x\) . Hence, each of the random variables  \(\tau _1, \tau _2 - \tau _1', \dots , \tau _{m+1} - \tau _m'\)  stochastically dominates an exponential random variable with rate  \( c_\textrm{max} \, k \, \hat{\alpha }_\textrm{max} \) . Thus, letting  \(\tau _1^*,\tau _2^*, \dots \)  be some i.i.d. exponentials of rate  \( c_\textrm{max} \, k \, \hat{\alpha }_\textrm{max}\) , by Markovianity we get $$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [ \tau _{m+1} \le t+\delta ]&\le \mathbb {P}\, [ \tau _1^* + \cdots + \tau _{m+1}^* \le t + \delta ] \\&= \mathbb {P}\, [\textrm{Gamma} \,(m+1, c_\textrm{max} \, k \, \hat{\alpha }_\textrm{max} ) \le t+\delta ] \xrightarrow {m \rightarrow \infty } 0 \; \text {,}\;\;\end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$ 
                    (A.11)
                 where  \(\textrm{Gamma}\,(u,\beta )\)  denotes the Gamma distribution with shape  \(u>0\)  and rate  \(\beta >0\) . Therefore, by ( A.9 ), ( A.10 ) and ( A.11 ), we conclude that $$\begin{aligned} \limsup _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [T_\varepsilon (t+\delta ) \le t ]&\le \limsup _{m \rightarrow \infty } \limsup _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \frac{m^2 \,{C_1}\, \varepsilon \, \log k}{ \delta \, c_\textrm{min}} \\&+ \limsup _{m \rightarrow \infty } \mathbb {P}\, [\textrm{Gamma}\,(m+1, c_\textrm{max} \, k \, \hat{\alpha }_\textrm{max} ) \le t+\delta ] = 0 \; \text {,}\;\;\end{aligned}$$ as desired.  \(\square \) 
                
                   Lemma A.4 
                   We have $$\begin{aligned} \lim _{\delta \rightarrow 0} \lim _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \sup _{\xi \in \Omega _k} \mathbb {P}_\xi ^\varepsilon \, [ \tau _{\Delta _k} < 3\delta ] = 0 \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ 
                
                   Proof 
                   As explained in the proof of Lemma  A.3 , starting from any  \(\xi \in \Omega _k\) ,  \(\tau _{\Delta _k}\)  stochastically dominates an exponential random variable of rate  \(c_\textrm{max}\, k\, \hat{\alpha }_\textrm{max}\) . Thus, we get $$\begin{aligned} \limsup _{\delta \rightarrow 0} \sup _{\varepsilon \in (0,1)} \mathbb {P}_\xi ^\varepsilon \,[ \tau _{\Delta _k} < 3\delta ] \le \limsup _{\delta \rightarrow 0} \left( 1 - e^{- c_\textrm{max} \,k\, \hat{\alpha }_\textrm{max} \,3\delta } \right) = 0 \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ This concludes the proof.  \(\square \) 
                 Now, we are ready to prove Proposition  A.1 . 
                   Proof of Proposition A.1 
                   We prove $$\begin{aligned} \lim _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [ \eta ^\varepsilon (t) \in \Omega _k ] =1\; \text {,}\;\;\quad t>0\; \text {,}\;\;\eta \in \Xi _k \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ 
                    (A.12)
                 We follow the proof ideas of [ LLM18 , Lemma 3.1]. By ( A.8 ), for  \(\delta \in (0,t/3)\) , we estimate $$\begin{aligned} 1 = \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [ \eta ^\varepsilon (S_\varepsilon (t-3\delta )) \in \Omega _k ] \le&\ \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \left[ \eta ^\varepsilon (S_\varepsilon (t-3\delta )) \in \Omega _k, \ S_\varepsilon (t-3\delta ) \in [t-3\delta , t-2\delta ] \right] \\&+ \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \,[ S_\varepsilon (t-3\delta ) > t-2\delta ] \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ By Lemma  A.3 , the second probability in the right-hand side vanishes as  \(\varepsilon \rightarrow 0\) . Moreover, by ( A.8 ), the first probability in the right-hand side is bounded by $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [ \, \exists s \in [t-3\delta , t-2\delta ], \ \eta ^\varepsilon (s) \in \Omega _k \, ] \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ We can further divide this into two events and bound the last probability as $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \,[ \eta ^\varepsilon (t) \in \Omega _k ] + \mathbb {P}_\eta ^\varepsilon \, [ \, \exists s \in [t-3\delta , t-2\delta ], \ \eta ^\varepsilon (s) \in \Omega _k, \ \eta ^\varepsilon (t) \in \Delta _k \, ] \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ Then, by the Markov property, the second probability above is bounded by $$\begin{aligned} \sup _{\xi \in \Omega _k} \mathbb {P}_\xi ^\varepsilon \, [ \tau _{\Delta _k} < 3\delta ] \; \text {,}\;\;\end{aligned}$$ which, by Lemma  A.4 , vanishes by first taking the limit  \(\varepsilon \rightarrow 0\) , and then  \(\delta \rightarrow 0\) . Collecting all displayed inequalities, we conclude that ( A.12 ) holds.  \(\square \) 
                 Appendix B. Reversibility of the limit process In this appendix, we prove Lemma  5.9  and simply write  \(\varsigma _{\hat{\alpha },k}=\varsigma _{\hat{\alpha },k,m}\) ,  \(k\ge m\) . 
                   Proof of Lemma 5.9 
                   We start by introducing an auxiliary continuous-time Markov chain on  \(\Xi _k\)  with rate function (cf. ( 5.1 )–( 5.5 )) $$\begin{aligned} \textbf{s}(\eta ,\,\cdot \,):={\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \textbf{r}^{\mathcal {A}}_{\hat{\alpha },k}(\eta ,\,\cdot \,) & \text {if}\quad \eta \in \Omega _k\; \text {,}\;\;\\ \textbf{r}^{\mathcal {B}}_k(\eta ,\,\cdot \,) & \text {if}\quad \eta \in \Delta _k\; \text {.}\end{array}\right. } \end{aligned}$$ 
                    (B.1)
                 In words, the above dynamics follows the rule of  \(A_{\hat{\alpha },k}\)  in  \(\Omega _k\) , while that of  \(B_k\)  in  \(\Delta _k\) . (Remark that this is not a  \(\textrm{SIP}\) -dynamics.) Now, recall the definition of  \(\Omega _{k,m}\)  from ( 5.10 ) and, similarly, introduce the set $$\begin{aligned} \Delta _{k,m} :=\left\{ \zeta \in \Delta _k: |\{x\in V: \zeta _x > 0 \}| = m \right\} \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ Observe that this auxiliary chain can jump from  \(\Omega _{k,m}\)  to either  \(\Delta _{k,m}\)  or  \(\Delta _{k,m+1}\)  (if  \(m\le k-1\) ), but a jump from  \(\Omega _{k,m}\)  to  \(\Delta _{k,m}\)  has rate zero to backtrack. 
                   Now, fix  \(m\le k-1\) . We claim that this dynamics, restricted to  \(\Omega _{k,m}\cup \Delta _{k,m+1}\) , is reversible with respect to  \(\varsigma _{\hat{\alpha },k}\) . For this purpose, it suffices to consider pairs  \(\eta , \xi = \eta -\delta _x+\delta _y \in \Omega _{k,m}\cup \Delta _{k,m+1}\)  (hence,  \(\eta _x\ge 1\) ); rates are zero otherwise. If  \(\eta \in \Omega _{k,m}\)  and  \(\xi \in \Omega _{k,m}\)  (such that  \(\eta _x =1\) ), then $$\begin{aligned} \varsigma _{\hat{\alpha },k}(\eta ) \, \textbf{r}^\mathcal {A}_{\hat{\alpha },k} (\eta ,\xi ) = c_{xy} \, \eta _x \hat{\alpha }_y \, \prod _{\begin{array}{c} z\in V\\ \eta _z> 0 \end{array}} \frac{\hat{\alpha }_z}{\eta _z} = c_{xy} \, \hat{\alpha }_x \xi _y \, \prod _{\begin{array}{c} z\in V \\ \xi _z > 0 \end{array}} \frac{\hat{\alpha }_z}{\xi _z} = \varsigma _{\hat{\alpha },k} (\xi ) \, \textbf{r}^\mathcal {A}_{\hat{\alpha },k} ( \xi ,\eta ) \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ If  \(\eta \in \Omega _{k,m}\)  and  \(\xi \in \Delta _{k,m+1}\)  (such that  \(\eta _x \ge 2\) ), we have $$\begin{aligned} \varsigma _{\hat{\alpha },k}(\eta ) \, \textbf{r}^\mathcal {A}_{\hat{\alpha },k} (\eta ,\xi ) = c_{xy} \, \eta _x \hat{\alpha }_y \, \prod _{\begin{array}{c} z\in V\\ \eta _z> 0 \end{array}} \frac{\hat{\alpha }_z}{\eta _z} = c_{xy} \, \xi _x \xi _y \, \prod _{\begin{array}{c} z\in V \\ \xi _z > 0 \end{array}} \frac{\hat{\alpha }_z}{\xi _z} = \varsigma _{\hat{\alpha },k} (\xi ) \, \textbf{r}^\mathcal {B}_k ( \xi ,\eta ) \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ If  \(\eta ,\xi \in \Delta _{k,m+1}\) , we have $$\begin{aligned} \varsigma _{\hat{\alpha },k}(\eta ) \, \textbf{r}^\mathcal {B}_k (\eta ,\xi ) = c_{xy} \, \eta _x \eta _y \, \prod _{\begin{array}{c} z\in V \\ \eta _z> 0 \end{array}} \frac{\hat{\alpha }_z}{\eta _z} = c_{xy} \, \xi _x \xi _y \, \prod _{\begin{array}{c} z\in V \\ \xi _z > 0 \end{array}} \frac{\hat{\alpha }_z}{\xi _z} = \varsigma _{\hat{\alpha },k} (\xi )\, \textbf{r}^\mathcal {B}_k ( \xi ,\eta ) \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ This shows that  \(\varsigma _{\hat{\alpha },k}\)  is reversible for the Markov chain described in ( B.1 ), restricted to  \(\Omega _{k,m}\cup \Delta _{k,m+1}\) . Furthermore, by ( 5.8 ), we have, for all distinct  \(\eta ,\xi \in \Omega _{k,m}\) , $$\begin{aligned} \varsigma _{\hat{\alpha },k}(\eta ) \, \textbf{r}^\mathcal {M}_{\hat{\alpha },k} (\eta ,\xi ) = \varsigma _{\hat{\alpha },k}(\eta ) \sum _{\zeta \in \Delta _{k,m+1}} \textbf{r}^\mathcal {A}_{\hat{\alpha },k} (\eta ,\zeta ) \, \textbf{P}_\zeta ^{\mathcal {B}} \, [\tau _\xi = \tau _{\Omega _k}] \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ Following  \((\mathcal {B}_k(t))_{t\ge 0}\) , from  \(\zeta \in \Delta _{k,m+1}\)  to arrive at  \(\xi \in \Omega _{k,m}\) , the trajectory must stay in  \(\Delta _{k,m+1}\) . Thus, by reversibility, the right-hand side is equal to $$\begin{aligned} \varsigma _{\hat{\alpha },k}(\xi ) \sum _{\zeta \in \Delta _{k,m+1}} \textbf{r}^\mathcal {A}_{\hat{\alpha },k} (\xi ,\zeta ) \, \textbf{P}_\zeta ^{\mathcal {B}} \, [\tau _\eta = \tau _{\Omega _k}] = \varsigma _{\hat{\alpha },k}(\xi ) \, \textbf{r}^\mathcal {M}_{\hat{\alpha },k} (\xi ,\eta ) \; \text {.}\end{aligned}$$ This concludes the proof of the lemma.  \(\square \) 
                 Notation guide . For the reader’s convenience, we collect some recurring notation. The table is only meant as a quick reference; all objects are defined in detail at the indicated locations. Symbol Brief description Ref. \(G=(V,(c_{xy})_{x,y\in V})\) Finite connected weighted graph;  \(c_{xy}=c_{yx}\ge 0\)  conductance of edge  xy Section  2 \(\alpha \) Site weights;  \(\alpha _{\min }:=\min _x\alpha _x\) ,  \(\alpha _{\max }:=\max _x\alpha _x\) ,  \(\alpha _\textrm{ratio}:=\alpha _{\min }/\alpha _{\max }\) ( 2.5 ) \(\Xi _k\) k -particle configurations;  \(\Xi _k=\{\eta \in \mathbb {N}_0^V:|\eta |=k\}\) Section  2 \(L_{G,\alpha ,k}\) Generator of the conservative process  \(\textrm{SIP}_k(G,\alpha )\)  on  \(\Xi _k\) ( 2.1 ) \(\mu _{\alpha ,k}\) Reversible Dirichlet-Multinomial measure of  \(\textrm{SIP}_k(G,\alpha )\) ( 2.2 ) \(\mathcal {E}_{G,\alpha ,k}\) Dirichlet form of  \(L_{G,\alpha ,k}\)  in  \(L^2(\mu _{\alpha ,k})\) ( 3.7 ) \(\textrm{gap}_k(G,\alpha )\) Spectral gap of the  k -particle conservative process  \(\textrm{SIP}_k(G,\alpha )\) Section  2.2 \(\textrm{gap}_\textrm{RW}(G,\alpha )\) One-particle gap;  \(\textrm{gap}_\textrm{RW}(G,\alpha )=\textrm{gap}_1(G,\alpha )\) ( 2.3 ) \(\textrm{gap}_\textrm{SIP}(G,\alpha )\) Conservative interacting gap;  \(\textrm{gap}_\textrm{SIP}(G,\alpha ):=\inf _{k\ge 1}\textrm{gap}_k(G,\alpha )\) ( 2.4 ) \(\mathfrak a_k\) Annihilation operator;  \((k-1)\) -particle observables  \(\longrightarrow \)   k -particle observables ( 3.2 ) \(\mathfrak a^\dagger _{\alpha ,k-1}\) Creation operator; adjoint of  \(\mathfrak a_k\)  in the corresponding  \(L^2(\mu _{\alpha ,k})\)  spaces ( 3.3 ) \(\alpha =\varepsilon \hat{\alpha }\) Small-diffusivity scaling; fixed direction  \(\hat{\alpha }\) , parameter  \(\varepsilon \downarrow 0\) ( 2.8 ) \(A_{\hat{\alpha },k}\) Slow part of  \(\varepsilon ^{-1}L_{G,\varepsilon \hat{\alpha },k}\) ; diffusive moves of particles ( 5.1 ) \(B_k\) Fast part of  \(\varepsilon ^{-1}L_{G,\varepsilon \hat{\alpha },k}\) ; attractive interaction/coalescence mechanism ( 5.1 ) \(\Omega _k\) Absorbing set of  \(B_k\) ; no two occupied sites are neighboring ( 5.4 ) \(\Delta _k\) Bad/transient complement;  \(\Delta _k:=\Xi _k\setminus \Omega _k\) ( 5.5 ) \(\Pi _k\) \(B_k\) -harmonic projection;  \(\Pi _k f=\lim _{t\rightarrow \infty }e^{tB_k}f\) Section  5.1 \({\mathscr {G}}_{\hat{\alpha },k}\) Effective slow–fast generator  \(\Pi _k A_{\hat{\alpha },k}\)  on  \(\mathcal {R}(\Pi _k)\subset \mathbb {R}^{\Xi _k}\) Proposition  5.1 \(w_k(\hat{\alpha })\) Spectral gap of the limiting metastable dynamics generated by  \({\mathscr {G}}_{\hat{\alpha },k}\) ( 5.7 ) \(M_{\hat{\alpha },k}\) Rate matrix on  \(\Omega _k\)  associated with  \({\mathscr {G}}_{\hat{\alpha },k}: \mathcal {R}(\Pi _k)\rightarrow \mathcal {R}(\Pi _k)\) ( 5.9 ) \(r^M_{\hat{\alpha },k}\) Jump rates of the effective chain  \(M_{\hat{\alpha },k}\)  on  \(\Omega _k\) ( 5.8 ) \(\Omega _{k,m}\) States in  \(\Omega _k\)  with exactly  m  separated stacks ( 5.10 ) \(M_{\hat{\alpha },k,m}\) Restriction of  \(M_{\hat{\alpha },k}\)  to  \(\Omega _{k,m}\) ; transient block for  \(m\ge 2\) ( 5.11 ) \(\lambda _{k,m}(\hat{\alpha })\) Principal eigenvalue of  \(-M_{\hat{\alpha },k,m}\) ( 5.12 ) \(\Xi \) Full non-conservative space;  \(\Xi =\mathbb {N}_0^V=\cup _{k\ge 0}\Xi _k\) Section  6 \(\omega ,\theta \) Reservoir parameters;  \(\omega _x \leftrightarrow \)  interaction rate w/ res. at  x ;  \(\theta _x \leftrightarrow \)  density res. at  x Section  6 \(L_{G,\alpha ,\omega ,\theta }\) Generator of the non-conservative/open  \(\textrm{SIP}(G,\alpha ,\omega ,\theta )\) ( 6.1 ) \(\nu _{\alpha ,\varrho }\) Product reversible measure for the open SIP when  \(\theta \equiv \varrho >0\) ( 6.2 ) \(L_{G,\alpha ,\omega ,k}\) Generator of the  k -particle absorbing system; case  \(\theta \equiv 0\) ( 7.1 ) \(\textrm{gap}_k(G,\alpha ,\omega )\) Lowest eigenvalue of  \(-L_{G,\alpha ,\omega ,k}\)  for the  k -particle absorbing system Section  6.1 \(\textrm{gap}_\textrm{RW}(G,\alpha ,\omega )\) One-particle killed gap; killing rate  \(\omega _x\)  at site  x ( 6.4 ) \(\textrm{gap}_\textrm{SIP}(G,\alpha ,\omega )\) Absorbing interacting gap;  \(\textrm{gap}_\textrm{SIP}(G,\alpha ,\omega ):=\inf _{k\ge 1}\textrm{gap}_k(G,\alpha ,\omega )\) ( 6.3 ) \(\textrm{gap}_\textrm{SIP}(G,\alpha ,\omega ,\varrho )\) First  \(L^2(\nu _{\alpha ,\varrho })\)  gap of the reversible open SIP; case  \(\theta \equiv \varrho >0\) Cor.  6.4 \(D_{\alpha ,\varrho }\) Orth. poly. duality functions; finite-particle absorbing systems  \(\longrightarrow \)  open system ( 6.5 ) \(F^\psi _{\alpha ,\varrho }\) Polynomial lift of  \(\psi \)  from  \(\Xi _k\)  to  \(\Xi \)  via  \(D_{\alpha ,\varrho }\) ( 6.7 ) \(\mathfrak b^\theta _{\alpha ,\omega ,\varrho ,k-1}\) Lowering operator in the lifting relation;
